=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
On a circle, there are N equally spaced points numbered 0,1,\ldots,N-1 in this order, with Alice at point 0 and Bob at point K. Initially, all points are colored white. Starting with Alice, they alternately perform the following operation:

- Choose one of the currently white points and color it black. Here, after the operation, the coloring of the points must be symmetric with respect to the straight line connecting the operator and the center of the circle.

If the operator cannot perform an operation satisfying the above condition, the sequence of operations ends there.
Both players cooperate and make the best choices to maximize the total number of points colored black in the end. Determine whether all points are colored black at the end of the sequence of operations.
You are given T test cases to solve.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
T
\mathrm{case}_1
\mathrm{case}_2
\vdots 
\mathrm{case}_T

Each test case \mathrm{case}_i (1 \leq i \leq T) is in the following format:
N K

Output

Print T lines. The i-th line should contain Yes if all points can be colored black for the i-th test case, and No otherwise.

Constraints


- 1 \leq T \leq 10^5
- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq K \leq N-1
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4
6 2
6 3
6 1
200000 100000

Sample Output 1

Yes
No
Yes
No

For N=6 and K=2, all points can be colored black by, for example, performing operations in the following order:

- Alice colors point 3 black.
- Bob colors point 1 black.
- Alice colors point 5 black.
- Bob colors point 2 black.
- Alice colors point 4 black.
- Bob colors point 0 black.


For N=6 and K=3, below is one possible progression. Actually, no matter what they do, they cannot color all points black.

- Alice colors point 3 black.
- Bob colors point 0 black.
- Alice cannot color any point black so that the coloring will be symmetric with respect to her line, so she cannot perform the operation.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Similarly Alice transitions from B-sym to A-sym.

Thus the process is: Starting empty (both sym). Alice adds a fixed point of A. Then Bob from A-sym S adds either a fixed point of B if S is B-sym, or completes a B-pair if S has one defect. After Bob, S is B-sym. Then Alice similarly.

Let's maybe derive a direct formula from g:
```
import math
def solve():
    import sys
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    T=data[0]
    out=[]
    idx=1
    for _ in range(T):
        N=data[idx]; K=data[idx+1]; idx+=2
        if N % 2 == 1:
            ok = math.gcd(N, K) == 1
        elif N % 4 == 2:
            ok = math.gcd(N//2, K) == 1
        else:
            ok = False
        out.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    print("\n".join(out))
```

Now, let's decide final code. Use g formula:
```
import sys, math

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    ans = []
    idx = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = data[idx]; K = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        g = math.gcd(N, 2*K)
        if g <= 2 and (N // g) % 2 == 1:
            ans.append("Yes")
        else:
            ans.append("No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

200000 100000: g=gcd(200000,200000)=200000 >2 -> No.
Matches.

Now, let's maybe attempt to prove the g formula from another angle to ensure no off-by-one in g definition. We defined C = B∘A = translation by 2K. The cycles of C are residue classes modulo g = gcd(N,2K). A and B map residue r to -r. Fixed points of A/B lie in self-negative residues. If g>2, there are non-self-negative residues, impossible. If g=1 or2, all residues self-negative. The length of each C-cycle is L=N/g. The deterministic sequence fills a cycle in L moves. Need L odd. This exactly gives g≤2 and L odd. Good.

Let's verify for g=2,L odd, the two C-cycles are both self-negative. Are they both invariant under A and B? A maps residue r to -r; since r=0 or1 and -0=0, -1=1 mod2, yes. B also maps r to -r because 2K≡0 mod2? Actually B(x)=2K-x ≡ -x ≡ x mod2 (since -1≡1), so yes. Good.

For g=1, only residue0, trivially invariant.

Now, let's think about the deterministic sequence in terms of C-cycle length L. Starting with A-fixed point 0, the sequence of added points is 0, C(0), C^{-1}(0), C^2(0), C^{-2}(0), ... This is because:
- Alice starts with fixed point p (A p=p).
- Bob adds B(p) = C A(p) = C(p).
- Alice adds A(C(p)) = C^{-1} A(p) = C^{-1}(p).
- Bob adds B(C^{-1}(p)) = C A C^{-1}(p). Need compute equals C^2(p)? Let's verify with p=0: C^{-1}(p) = -s; B(-s)=s - (-s)=2s = C^2(0). In general, B C^{-1} = C^2 A? Let's compute: B = C A. B C^{-1} = C A C^{-1}. In dihedral, A C^{-1} = C A? Wait A C = C^{-1} A, so A C^{-1} = C A. Thus B C^{-1} = C (C A) = C^2 A. Applied to p (A p=p) gives C^2(p). Yes. So sequence alternates C^j and C^{-j}. It covers the cycle if L odd; if L even, it still covers but final parity differs. Actually for any L, the sequence 0, C(0), C^{-1}(0), C^2(0), C^{-2}(0), ... up to C^{L/2}(0) covers all. The number of moves is L. The final move parity is Alice if L odd, Bob if L even. This is a clean proof.

For a B-fixed start, analogous with C maybe.

```
import sys
from math import gcd

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    out = []
    idx = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = data[idx]
        K = data[idx+1]
        idx += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1):
            out.append("Yes")
        else:
            out.append("No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
Use `& 1` for odd. Fine.

Now, let's maybe think if the final answer should be exactly:
```python
import sys
from math import gcd

def main():
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    T = next(it)
    ans = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = next(it); K = next(it)
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        ans.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    print("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Suppose block B = C_r ∪ C_{-r}, r≠-r. It is invariant under both A and B. If S∩B=∅, and a player colors x∈B, then after move, (S∪{x})∩B = {x}. Maybe we can prove by induction that before E is full, every move is in E. Let's formalize:

Let E be the cycle containing A-fixed points (even). Let O be the other. Since O is empty until first O move, the global symmetry condition is equivalent to E being symmetric under the current axis (because O empty symmetric). We claim:
- After Alice's move, if E is not full, E is A-symmetric but not B-symmetric.
- After Bob's move, if E is not full, E is B-symmetric but not A-symmetric.
Base: Alice's first move colors an A-fixed point in E, so E A-sym. Since E has no B-fixed points (K odd,L even), the colored point is not B-fixed, so E not B-sym. Bob forced to color in E. This induction continues. Therefore before E full, no move in O. E has L points, so after L moves from start it becomes full. Since L even, the move that fills E is Bob's (move L). Actually in first cycle starting with Alice, Bob moves at even positions 2,4,...,L-1 (since L odd, L-1 even). Bob never makes the final move in that cycle. B-invariant sets after Bob moves have even sizes: 2,4,...,L-1. The final Alice move makes size L odd A-symmetric. Good. Second cycle starting with Bob, Bob moves at odd offsets? Starting move M even, sequence moves: Bob (size1), Alice(size2), Bob(size3), ... Since L odd, final Bob size L odd. B-invariant sets after Bob moves have odd sizes because B has a fixed point. Good. This is consistent.

Now, let's maybe think if there is any possibility that the final code should be:
```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

```
```
def solve():
    import sys
    from math import gcd
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

```
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    t = int(data[0])
    out = []
    p = 1
    for _ in range(t):
        N = int(data[p]); K = int(data[p+1]); p += 2
        g = gcd(N, 2*K)
        out.append("Yes" if g <= 2 and ((N // g) & 1) else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```
